2.   RECOGNITION  
A.  TSD Mission Moment — Troy College & Career Dual Enrollment Students 
Tonight, we honor a special group of high school s tudents who are enrolled in 
college classes while completing their high school course requirements.  The Dual 
Enrollment program at Troy College & Career High School has greatly expanded 
from eight students who, over nine years ago, started classes at Baker Community 
College while attending Niles Community High School.  Currently, 44 students 
(including nine from Intersect) are enrolled in between one and four classes at 
Oakland and Macomb Community Colleges and even Oakland University while 
completing high school course requirements. 
Nearly thirty percent of Troy College & Career students (both in and outside of the 
Troy School District) are currently enrolled in college.  The six students who are 
being recognized this evening exemplify the spectrum of learners we ser ve -  
varying from one who is finishing her second year at OCC to our newest student 
who is taking his first college course.  They represent many who are choosing to 
attend TCC specifically for its dual enrollment opportunities.  
These students all compl eted a College Prep course to get them ready for post -
secondary education and now are enrolled in our College Seminar class in which 
they receive personal assistance with time management and class organization, 
content areas, essay writing, study and test -taking, educational career planning, 
learning how to apply for scholarships, monitoring of grades, and much more.  
Additionally, collaboration with their peers allows for a totally supportive 
environment for academic achievement!  We are very proud of these students who 
personify many others who are preparing to take college classes next year and 
those who are currently enjoying collegial success!   
Now, we would like to invite the following students up to be recognized by the 
Board.  
________________________________________________     
Recommended Action:  Invite students up to congratulate them.

2.       RECOGNITION 
B. Reading Recovery Teacher Leader Certification  
– Kristin Piotrowski 
The teacher leader candidate attends a registered university training center in the 
United States or a regional institute in Canada for an academic year of full-time 
professional development. Their complex role requires them to  
 become Reading Recovery teachers. 
 develop an academic understanding of the theoretical concepts upon which 
Reading Recovery is based and a flexibility to consider new concepts and 
practices. 
 test practice against theoretical concepts. 
 critically appraise Reading Recovery’s strengths and problem spots as well as 
competing explanations for its success. 
 observe and work through the experiences of a teacher trainee group across an 
academic year with the support of experienced teacher leaders in the field. 
 help teachers develop competency in individualized assessment-based instruction 
to improve student achievement. 
 become skilled at working with adult learners in order to effect significant change 
in teachers’ practice. 
 develop a thorough knowledge of the whole operation of Reading Recovery in an 
education system including organizing and administering the teacher training 
course and evaluating and reporting student outcomes. 
 become skilled at working with local administrators (e.g., site coordinator, 
principals). 
 develop interactive system-level leadership skills. 
In order to accomplish these goals during the initial year of professional learning, 
teacher leader candidates teach four Reading Recovery students daily; participate in 
graduate-level classes that include teaching sessions; engage in course work to 
explore theoretical concepts in reading, writing, language, literacy difficulties, and 
adult learning theory; participate in leadership seminars and practica that include 
field work at established sites; and prepare their home districts for Reading Recovery 
implementation. 
4
As a result of Kristin’s intense year-long training, Troy School District will be one of 
15 sites in Michigan for Reading Recovery teachers.  We look forward to Kristin’s 
expertise impacting our Reading Recovery teachers and all students in the Troy 
School District.  This is exciting work that will take our Troy School District literacy 
work to world class status.  Thank you to Kristin for her hard work and dedication to 
her studies over the last year and years to come. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:   Invite Kristin up to receive her certificate.

Troy Board of Education Meeting 2 February 28, 2017 
11
All-State Athletes Fall & Winter 
2016-2017 
Writing Assignment 
The Board recognized All-State athletes for the fall and 
winter of 2016 – 2017.  The students recognized were: 
Tonight, as part of our Troy School District Mission Moment, 
we recognize 3 
rd  grader, Katarina Tasich, for her efforts to 
make the world a better place and taking a stand against 
bullying. During the month of December, Troy Union 
students wrote opinion pieces on a variety of topics.  The 
students were excited about the project and wanted their 
voices to be heard.  Many of them reached out to th e 
principal.  One topic that peeked Mr. Cottone’s interest was 
Katarina’s essay – she took a risk and shared that our 
community should do more to stop bullying.  A few days 
later Katarina formally shared her writing piece with him 
and outlined the steps that could be done.  The ideas were 
great but one stood out.  She suggested that the entire 
school wear black around a unified message of “Blacking out 
Bullying.”  The message caught on at Troy Union and  
throughout the entire school district as many community 
members wore black to support her message.  Katrina is 
proof that we can all make a difference.  Mr. Cottone was 
asked to say a few words and he invited Katrina and her 
teacher, Jess Bronsteen, to join him to share more about 
Katrina’s essay on blocking out bullying.  The Board 
congratulated Katrina for her efforts. 
National Blue Ribbon Award – 
this evening for their designation as a National Blue Ribbon 
School. This award honors Schroeder as one of the very best 
schools in the United States.  
U.S. Secretary of Education John B. King, Jr. recognized 
Schroeder in a ceremony in Washington, D. C. this past 
November. Just 329 schools from around the country were 
chosen for this honor in 2016. This award affirms the hard 
work of students, staff, parents, and the Schroeder 
community.  As Secretary King stated, "National Blue Ribbon 
Schools are proof that we can prepare every child for 
college and meaningful careers.  Your schools are on the 
cutting edge, pioneering innovative educational practices—
professional learning communities, project-based learning, 
social and emotional learning, positive behavior systems—
making you shining examples for your communities, your 
state and the nation."  Mr. Canfield was invited to come up 
and introduced a few members of the Schroeder staff that 
were in attendance.   The Board congratulated everyone for 
their achievement and recognition by the State. 
Troy Board of Education Meeting 3 February 28, 2017 
12
Schroeder Elementary School 
We are pleased to recognize Schroeder Elementary School 
Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate 
to have an exemplary group of principals dedicated to the 
success of every student in the District. This evening, we will 
pass a resolution to commend our elementary and 
secondary principals for their exceptional commitment to 
excellence in the Troy School District.  
It was moved by Dr. Philippart and supported by Mrs . 
Fleming that WHEREAS,  energetic and inspiring school 
leadership is essential if teachers, schools and districts are 
to ensure that all Troy students are college and career 
ready; and, 
WHEREAS,  K-12 school principals play a vital role in the 
success of students and act as the liaison between the 
school and the community it serves, ensuring that parents 
and the community are aware of student and school 
achievements; and, 
WHEREAS,  Troy School District principals are 
dedicated, quality educational professionals committed to 
providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff and 
students; and,  
WHEREAS,  during this month, we join with educators, 
parents and students throughout Michigan to raise 
awareness of the importance of educational leadership, and 
to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for 
their exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED,  that the Troy Board of 
Education officially recognizes February as Principal 
Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks our dedicated  
building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
Yes:        7 
No:         0

Dr. Machesky provided a report on enrollment projections 
most likely and high and then explained how the 
projections are calculated for enrollment using that

Schools Closed to Open 
Education adopts a resolution to close Bemis, Schroeder and 
Boulan Park Schools as well as Athens and Troy High Schools 
to open enrollment for the 2017-2018 school year, with the 
exception of not more than (25) twenty-five incoming ninth 
grade students who reside in the Troy High School 
attendance area, but wish to attend Athens High School.  
Students with siblings attending Athens and Troy High and 
currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed to open 
enroll to remain for the 2017-2018 school year.

REPORT on Schools of Choice 
Learning provided a brief report to the Board for 
consideration of the Schools of Choice Recommendations 
for the 2017-2018 school year.  He explained that many 
factors were taken into consideration when making this 
recommendation such as:  current enrollment, enrollment 
projections, patterns and trends, numbers and percentages 
of Schools of Choice students currently in the district, 
projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would 
be age-eligible to enter the district in 2017-2018 and 
available space in classrooms.  A brief PowerPoint was 
shared as well.  Mr. Dziatczak also commented that the

Mr. Hauff asked how the end of life for a bus is determined.

2004 and the average life of a bus is 12 years so the plan is 
to replace them every 12-15 years.  He added that money 
was set aside from the 2013/14 bond.

performing Guys & Dolls for their spring musical starting 
be held this Thursday evening.  Pranathi also shared that 
their annual culture show is coming up and will feature 
dances, songs, and food from different countries.  Pranathi 
announced that the gymnastics team qualified for the state 
competition and the that the synchronized swim team will 
be going to the state meet soon.

also mentioned the successful MLK Day event.   Ellory 
indicated that four Athens teachers were nominated for 
Teacher of the Year and that Mrs. Pierce won overal l 
teacher of the year!  Ellory also announced that the Athens 
spring play is High School Musical and invited everyone to 
see it.

           A.    CONSIDERATION:       2016 - 17 Budget Amendment Resolution

Fund - Amendment 2, Food Service Fund - Amendment 1, Troy Career Center - Amendment 1, Adult 
Education and Community Service Fund - Amendment 1, Capital Projects Funds - Amendment 1, and 
the Capital Maintenance Fund - Amendment 1.  Approval of the attached resolution is recommended.
We do not have to read the Budget Amendment Resolution in its entirety.  However, it must be made a 
part of the official minutes.

A RFP was issued on February 15, 2017 to obtain competitive 
bids for the Larson Office Renovation, IAE Storage Room and Interior/Exterior Door 
Replacements at multiple buildings.  The scope of work for this RFP is to renovate the old 
office area at Larson into a collaborative teaching & learning space, create a storage area in 
the old pool shower area at IAE and remove and replace interior & exterior doors at Barnard, 
Bemis & Wass Elementary Schools, Larson Middle School, Athens & Troy High Schools and the 
Administration Building.

bids are detailed in the bid tally sheet attached.  I am recommending the following vendors 
based on low bid for their category.

An RFP (Bid 9835) was issued to obtain competitive bids  for site work at Troy High School, 
Boulan Park Middle School, Bemis, Hamilton & Hill Elementary Schools and the Administration 
& Services Buildings .  The scope of work for this RFP is to perform parking lot

the Administration & Services Buildings; it also included parking lot replacement of the Boulan 
Park bus loop, detention pond grading at Troy High School & site lighting replacement at 
Hamilton Elementary.  Site Lighting work will commence this spring and the remainder of the 
work will commence June 19, 2017 with a completion of August 2017.

An RFP was created to obtain competitive b ids for Interior Pai nting at Athens & Troy 
High Schools, Larson & Smith Middle Schools and Wattles Elementary School.   The 
scope of work for this RFP is to provide wall preparation and painting to Hallways, 
Classroom, Weight Room & Cafeteria perspective to each school as detailed in the bid 
specifications.  This work is part of our District Deferred Maintenance Painting 
Schedule. 
On February 27th  we opened  3 bids and Post Bid Interviews were performed by 
General Painting Company LLC being identified as the low qualified bidder (please see 
attached bid tally ).  Bradley Duane LLC (the apparent low bidder ) requested to 
rescind their bid due to incomplete scope of work .  General Painting has performed 
similar work for other school districts and universities in the Metro Detroit Area and 
provided references which were verified and positive. 
The cost for the base bid is $58,000 .00 and I am requesting a total project cost 
potential painting needs  can be identified and completed throughout the year with 
pricing based on labor rates obtained as part of this RFP .  The funding for this 
purchase will be from the Capital Maintenance Funds. 
Please let me know if you have any questions.

8. F.   03.21.17 
8. BUSINESS 
F. CONSIDERATION:  DISTRICT-OWNED PROPERTY 
 The District has been conducting pre -sale investigations of district -owned 
vacant property and offers this resolution for Board of Education 
consideration. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Approve the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
Whereas, the Troy School District Board of Education’s mission is to ensure learning for 
all members of the school community; and 
Whereas, the Troy School District Board of Education appreciates and values the real 
property owned by the District; and  
Whereas, the District desires to develop an Early Childhood Center to service our 
preschool programs; and  
Whereas, the Troy School District Board of Education desires to explore and consider 
the sale of certain surplus, vacant real property in order to offset long term debt for the 
development of an Early Childhood Center, and 
Whereas, the Troy School District Board of Education desires to authorize the School 
District’s Administration, its legal counsel, Clark Hill PLC, and its real estate consultant, 
Great Northern Consulting Group, to explore/market/analyze the sale of certain surplus, 
vacant real property , as outlined below , with the understanding that any 
Offer/Agreements etc. will be brought before the Board for approval, 
129
8. F.   03.21.17 
Now, Therefore, be it Resolved That:  
1. The School District  authorizes administration to further investigate the 
preservation of Section 1 property , with the exception of  the frontage on 
Square Lake which will be split into its own parcel and marketed for sale.  
The remainder of the land may be preserved in perpetuity  either by the School 
District or through a conservation easement or other similar form of agreement. 
2. The School District authorizes the marketing of Section 11 property for purposes 
of sale. 
3. The School District desires to develop an Early Childhood Center on approximately 
8 acres of Section 16 and preserve the balance of the subject property. 
4. The School District appoints TMP Architecture as the Architect of record and 
authorizes TMP Architecture to commence the design process  for an Early 
Childhood Center in accordance with their submitted  proposal for services dated 
March 10, 2017 .  The cost of services is  not to exceed $933,716  to complete 
phases 1 through 4 of the proposal  and is approved as an expenditure of the 
Capital Maintenance Fund.     
5. The cost associated with the selling of property is an approved expenditure of the 
Capital Maintenance Fund Professional Services Budget. 
6. Revenue generated from these property sales shall be restricted in the Capital 
Maintenance Fund for the purposes of using toward the development of the Early 
Childhood Center. 
Yes: ____________ 
No: ____________

RE: Troy School District – Early Childhood Center 
Dear Rick, 
Thank you for the opportunity to provide our proposal for the Troy Early Childhood Center  
It has been a pleasure to work with you to develop the project scope and initial concepts. Per your 
request, I have included a breakdown of our proposed fees, by phase, based on our previous agreement 
for the 2013 TSD Bond projects. The fee for architectural and engineering services is $1,167,144.00 and 
is based on 6% of the estimated construction cost of $19,452,406. The fee includes architectural, 
furniture, mechanical, electrical, civil and landscaping design services. The fee does not include 
technology consulting services or design. 
Fee by phase 
1.   Schematic design 15% $175,072 
2.   Design development 20% $233,429 
3.   Construction Documents 40% $466,858 
4.   Bidding 5% $58,357 
5.   Construction 18% $210,066 
6.   Close out 2%  $23,343 
Total  $1,167,144 
Please let me know if you have any question 
Sincerely, 
TMP Architecture, Inc. 
John Waldrop, AIA
